S5
Theorem: different projective classes lead to inequivalent direct-product representations

In this section we prove in detail the key theorem in the main text, which states that if two projective irReps
\rho_{1,2}
of
P\times{Z}_{2}^{T}
belong to different projective classes
\vec{\eta}(\rho_{1})\neq\vec{\eta}(\rho_{2})
, then
\rho_{1}\otimes\rho^{\ast}_{1}
and
\rho_{2}\otimes\rho^{\ast}_{2}
must be inequivalent.

As
\rho_{1}
and
\rho_{2}
belong to two different projective classes, at least one symmetry invariant must take different values for
\rho_{1}
and
\rho_{2}
.
Then there are two possibilities:

(i) there is one anti-unitary invariant
\eta_{a}
such that
\eta_{a}(\rho_{1})\neq\eta_{a}(\rho_{2})
, and

(ii) all anti-unitary invariants are same respectively, but one or more unitary invariants take different values, or symbolically
\eta_{a}(\rho_{1})=\eta_{a}(\rho_{2})
for all anti-unitary elements
a
, but
\eta_{u,v}(\rho_{1})\neq\eta_{u,v}(\rho_{2})
.

In fact, one can prove that for
P
being any crystallographic point group other than
C_{4h}
, each unitary invariant can be expressed in terms of several anti-unitary invariants.
This is because, according to Supplementary Table
S1
, if
P\neq{C}_{4h}
, for unitary invariant
\eta_{u,v}
,
we have
u^{2}=v^{2}=(uv)^{2}=E

and can easily prove that

\rho(uT)\rho^{\ast}(uT)\rho(vT)\rho^{\ast}(vT)\rho(T)\rho^{\ast}(T)=\rho(u)\rho(u)\rho(v)\rho(v)\rho(T)\rho^{\ast}(T)=\eta_{u,v}(\rho)\rho(uvT)\rho^{\ast}(uvT),

(S20)

which entails

\eta_{u,v}=\eta_{uT}\eta_{vT}\eta_{T}\eta_{uvT}.

(S21)

For example, if
P=D_{4h}
, one set of symmetry invariants can be defined by

\vec{\eta}=

(
\eta_{C_{2x},C_{2y}}
,

\eta_{T}
,

\eta_{IT}
,

\eta_{TC_{2x}}
,

\eta_{TC_{2a}}
,

\eta_{I,C_{2x}}
,

\eta_{I,C_{2a}}
)
in Supplementary Table
S1
.
From SEq.(
S21
),

\displaystyle\eta_{C_{2x},C_{2y}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta_{TC_{2x}}\eta_{TC_{2y}}\eta_{T}\eta_{TC_{2z}}=\eta_{TC_{2x}}^{2}\eta_{T}\eta_{TC_{2z}},

(S22)

\displaystyle\eta_{I,C_{2x}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta_{IT}\eta_{TC_{2x}}\eta_{T}\eta_{TM_{x}},

(S23)

\displaystyle\eta_{I,C_{2a}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta_{IT}\eta_{TC_{2a}}\eta_{T}\eta_{TM_{da}},

(S24)

so the unitary invariants
\eta_{C_{2x},C_{2y}}
,
\eta_{I,C_{2x}}
and

\eta_{I,C_{2a}}
can be replaced by the anti-unitary invariants
\eta_{TC_{2z}}
,
\eta_{TM_{x}}

and
\eta_{TM_{da}}
, respectively.
We obtain a new definition of symmetry invariants
\vec{\eta}=
(
\eta_{TC_{2z}}
,
\eta_{T}
,
\eta_{IT}
,
\eta_{TC_{2x}}
,

\eta_{TC_{2a}}
,
\eta_{TM_{x}}
,
\eta_{TM_{da}}
) of
D_{4h}\times Z_{2}^{T}
.

Therefore, for
P\neq{C}_{4h}
, if
\vec{\eta}(\rho_{1})\neq\vec{\eta}(\rho_{2})
, then there must be
an anti-unitary element
a
(
a^{2}=E
) such that
\eta_{a}(\rho_{1})\neq\eta_{a}(\rho_{2})

since all the symmetry invariants can be anti-unitary invariants.
This belongs to case-(i).

Now we want to prove that
\rho_{1}\otimes\rho_{1}^{\ast}
and
\rho_{2}\otimes\rho_{2}^{\ast}
are inequivalent Reps of
P\times{Z}_{2}^{T}
.
To do this, we first observe that we show that they are inequivalent Reps of
P\times{Z}_{2}^{T}
if they are inequivalent Reps of any subgroup of
P\times{Z}_{2}^{T}
.
Consider the subgroup
H=\{E,a\}
, which is the smallest group containing
a
.
The hermitian fermion bilinear operators considered in the main text can be combined into two linear Reps of
H
: (i) those that are even under
a
, namely
D^{(+)}(a)=1
; (ii) those that are odd under
a
, namely
D^{(-)}(a)=-1
.
Without loss of generality, suppose
\eta_{a}(\rho_{1})=-\eta_{a}(\rho_{2})=1
, then one can prove that
Yang et al. 2021b

\displaystyle\rho_{1}\otimes\rho_{1}^{\ast}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{d_{1}(d_{1}+1)}{2}D^{(+)}(a)\oplus\frac{d_{1}(d_{1}-1)}{2}D^{(-)}(a),

(S25)

\displaystyle\rho_{2}\otimes\rho_{2}^{\ast}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{d_{2}(d_{2}-1)}{2}D^{(+)}(a)\oplus\frac{d_{2}(d_{2}+1)}{2}D^{(-)}(a),

where
d_{1,2}
is the dimension of
\rho_{1,2}
.
From SEq.(
S25
), we see that the two direct-product Reps are inequivalent, independent of the values of
d_{1,2}
.
Following the previous argument, they must also be inequivalent Reps of
P\times{Z}_{2}^{T}
.

Now, if
P=C_{4h}
, there is a unitary invariant
\eta_{I,C^{+}_{4z}}
that cannot be expressed in terms of
\eta_{a}
.
So if
\rho_{1}
and
\rho_{2}
have
\eta_{a}(\rho_{1})=\eta_{a}(\rho_{2})
for all anti-unitary
a
(
a^{2}=E
), it is still possible that
\eta_{I,C^{+}_{4z}}(\rho_{1})\neq\eta_{I,C^{+}_{4z}}(\rho_{2})
.
For this case, we have computed all such pairs of
\rho_{1,2}
, and checked explicitly that, for all the pairs,
\rho_{1}\otimes\rho_{1}^{\ast}
and
\rho_{2}\otimes\rho_{2}^{\ast}
are inequivalent Reps of
C_{4h}\times{Z}_{2}^{T}
.

We conclude that the theorem holds for all
P\times{Z}_{2}^{T}
, where
P
is any crystallographic point group.

However, although this conclusion can be rigorously proved for groups
P\times Z_{2}^{T}
, it is not always valid if the symmetry group is not of the form
P\times Z_{2}^{T}
.
In this case, one may distinguish different projective classes
by lowering the symmetry with a background field.

S6
Comparison with 8-fold fermions in Magnetic space groups

For little co-group
G_{\mathbf{K}}=D_{4h}\times Z_{2}^{T}
,
{\rm Invariants}\equiv
(
\eta_{C_{2x},C_{2y}}
,
\eta_{T}
,
\eta_{IT}
,
\eta_{TC_{2x}}
,
\eta_{TC_{2a}}
,
\eta_{I,C_{2x}}
,
\eta_{I,C_{2a}}
), we compare three different 8-dimensional irReps coming from three projective classes of

D_{4h}\times Z_{2}^{T}
.

From Supplementary Table
S17
, for the set of invariants
\vec{\eta}=(-1,-1,-1,-1,+1,+1,-1)
colored in GREEN, the corresponding projective class can be realized at A in BZ of type II MSGs 130.424, 135.484 with spin-orbit coupling (SOC) and type IV MSGs 126.385,131.445 with SOC;
for the set of invariants
\vec{\eta}=(-1,+1,-1,-1,+1,-1,+1)
colored in BLUE, the corresponding projective class can be realized at A in BZ of type IV MSGs 125.374, 129.420,132.458,136.504 with SOC. The two sets of invariants lead to two different 8-fold fermions discussed in SRefs.
Bradlyn et al. 2016
and
Cano et al. 2019
.

While the set of invariants
\vec{\eta}=
(
-1
,
-1
,
+1
,
-1
,
+1
,
-1
,
+1
) colored in RED in Supplementary Table

also allow a 8-dimensional irRep which can be realized at A in BZ of SSGs

124.2.8.20
(isomorphic to type IV MSG
\mathcal{M}=126.385
),

132.2.8.28
(isomorphic to type IV MSG
\mathcal{M}=131.445
),

140.2.8.35
(isomorphic to type IV MSG
\mathcal{M}=125.374
),

129.2.8.15
(isomorphic to type IV MSG
\mathcal{M}=129.420
),

140.2.8.28
(isomorphic to type IV MSG
\mathcal{M}=132.458
),

127.2.8.14
(isomorphic to type IV MSG
\mathcal{M}=136.504
).
The elements of space group (SG)
\mathcal{L}_{0}\equiv Pb
(7) are pure lattice operations
of SSG 124.2.8.20,
and the elements of SG
\mathcal{L}_{0}\equiv Pm
(6) are pure lattice operations
of SSG 132.2.8.28.
The little co-group of SSGs 124.2.8.20 and 132.2.8.28 at high-symmetry point A
is

, which is generated by

and

as listed in Supplementary Table

.
The elements of SG

(2) are pure lattice operations of SSGs
140.2.8.35, 129.2.8.15, 140.2.8.28 and 127.2.8.14,
whose little co-group at high-symmetry point A
is
G_{\mathbf{K}}=D_{4h}\times Z_{2}^{T}
, which is generated by

(C_{2x}||C_{2x},M_{x})
,

,
(C_{2y}||C_{2y},M_{y})
and

(TC_{2z}||T,IT)
as listed in Supplementary Table
S5
.

Bradlyn et al. 2016

Cano et al. 2019

\displaystyle\rho_{1}(C_{2x})

(S28)

where

.

Figure S1:

.

We decompose
\rho_{1}\otimes\rho^{\ast}_{1}
,

into irReps of

(S29)

\displaystyle=

where

,

,

,
so the three 8-fold fermions have different response
to dispersion
E_{u}^{-}
, which can be linear dispersion

[k_{x},k_{y}]
.
From Eq.(

) of the main text,
linear irRep
\mu=E_{u}^{-}
,

\phi^{\mu}_{1}=k_{x}
,

\phi^{\mu}_{2}=k_{y}
.

takes the form

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle v_{11}\tau_{0}(k_{x}\sigma_{0}\sigma_{x}+k_{y}\sigma_{0}\sigma_{y})+(v_{12}\tau_{x}+v_{13}\tau_{y}+v_{14}\tau_{z})(k_{x}\sigma_{x}\sigma_{y}+k_{y}\sigma_{x}\sigma_{x}),

where the coupling parameters in Eq.(

) of the main text:

,

,

,

.

takes the form

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle v_{21}\tau_{z}(k_{x}\sigma_{0}\sigma_{x}+k_{y}\sigma_{0}\sigma_{y})+v_{22}\tau_{0}(k_{x}\sigma_{z}\sigma_{x}-k_{y}\sigma_{z}\sigma_{y}),

(S33)

where the coupling parameters in Eq.(
13
) of the main text:

\lambda^{(\mu)}_{1}=v_{21}
,

\lambda^{(\mu)}_{2}=v_{22}
.
And
the linear dispersion of 8-fold fermion (iii)
takes the form

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{3}=(v_{31}\tau_{x}+v_{32}\tau_{y}+v_{33}\tau_{z})(k_{x}\sigma_{0}\sigma_{x}+k_{y}\sigma_{0}\sigma_{y})+(v_{34}\tau_{x}+v_{35}\tau_{y}+v_{36}\tau_{z})(k_{x}\sigma_{x}\sigma_{x}-k_{y}\sigma_{x}\sigma_{y}),

(S34)

where the coupling parameters in Eq.(
13
) of the main text:

\lambda^{(\mu)}_{1}=v_{31}
,

\lambda^{(\mu)}_{2}=v_{32}
,

\lambda^{(\mu)}_{3}=v_{33}
,

\lambda^{(\mu)}_{4}=v_{34}
,

\lambda^{(\mu)}_{5}=v_{35}
,

.
Along high-symmetry lines
[100]
and

),(

)
of

S7
The SSG-only representation in candidate material
\textbf{Ce}_{3}\textbf{NIn}

Here we propose an SSG candidate material to realize the invariants of
G_{\mathbf{K}}=D_{4h}\times Z_{2}^{T}
,
namely (
\eta_{C_{2x},C_{2y}}
,
\eta_{T}
,
\eta_{IT}
,

\eta_{TC_{2x}}
,
\eta_{TC_{2a}}
,
\eta_{I,C_{2x}}
,
\eta_{I,C_{2a}}
).
From Supplementary Table
S17
, for the set of invariants
\vec{\eta}=(-1,-1,+1,+1,+1,-1,-1)

colored in RED,
the corresponding projective class is only realizable
in spin-space groups.
One possible SSG is

123.2.8.35
in the database
Jiang et al. 2024b
,
which is isomorphic to type IV MSG
\mathcal{M}=127.397
.
The elements of SG
\mathcal{L}_{0}=Pm
(6) are pure lattice operations.
The little co-group at high-symmetry points
\Gamma
,M,Z,A
is
G_{\mathbf{K}}=D_{4h}\times Z_{2}^{T}
, which is generated by

(E||E,M_{z})
,

(C_{2x}||C_{2x},M_{y})
,
(C_{2a}||C_{2a},M_{db})
,
(C_{2z}||C_{2z},I)
and

(T||T,TM_{z})
, where
I
is spacial inversion,
M
is mirror reflection plane.
The full symmetry of magnetic structure of
\mathrm{Ce}_{3}\mathrm{NIn}

Hellenes et al. 2024
in
Supplementary Fig.(S2) is exactly the non-coplanar SSG 123.2.8.35.
The symmetry invariants
\vec{\eta}=(-1,-1,+1,+1,+1,-1,-1)
can be realized at

\Gamma
,M,Z,A points in BZ and support 2-dimensional irRep generated by

\displaystyle\rho(T)K

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\sigma_{y}K,\rho(I)=i\sigma_{z},\rho(C_{2x})=i\sigma_{x},\rho(C_{2y})=i\sigma_{y},

\displaystyle\rho(C_{2a})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp[-\frac{i(\sigma_{x}+\sigma_{y})}{\sqrt{2}}\frac{\pi}{2}]=-\frac{i(\sigma_{x}+\sigma_{y})}{\sqrt{2}}.

(S35)

From SEq.(
S7
),
\rho(IT)K=i\sigma_{x}K
,

\eta_{IT}=[\rho(IT)K]^{2}=1
.
The band splitting around these points are linear dispersion in
k_{x},k_{y}
plane

\displaystyle\hat{H}(\mathbf{k})=k_{x}\sigma_{x}+k_{y}\sigma_{y}.

(S36)

The
\eta_{IT}=1
two-fold spinful fermion with linear dispersion in
k_{x},k_{y}
plane is unique in SSGs
since
\eta_{IT}=1
fermions in MSGs are spinless and carry single-valued irReps which cannot have linear band splitting in
k_{x},k_{y}
directions.

Figure S2:
Magnetic structure of
\mathrm{Ce}_{3}\mathrm{NIn}
with MAGNDATA
label
1.152

Gallego et al. 2016a
;
Gallego et al. 2016b
.
The red arrows on yellow balls indicate the directions of magnetic moments of
\mathrm{Ce}
atoms.
The SSG symmetry
is 123.2.8.35 in the database
Jiang et al. 2024b
.

S8
Quasiparticles in other spin-space groups

S8.1
Supplementary Table
S27
: Symmetry invariants for SSGs isomorphic to type III MSGs

For the non-coplanar SSGs isomorphic to type III MSGs, the little co-groups
G_{\mathbf{K}}
at high-symmetry point
\bf K

can be 58 type III magnetic point groups of the form

H+T(P-H)
, where
H
is a subgroup of the point group
P
containing half of the elements in
P
, the other half
P-H
followed by time reversal
T
.
Following Eqs.
(
8
)-(
11
)
of the main text, we calculate lattice-part invariants
\eta^{L}
and spin-part invariants
\eta^{S}
, then obtain the full invariants
\eta=\eta^{L}\eta^{S}
.
In Supplementary Table
S27
, we give definitions of symmetry invariants of
58 type III magnetic point groups.
For a type III magnetic point group, if not all the sets of symmetry invariants can be realized
in type III MSGs or at non-time-reversal-invariant high-symmetry points of type II and IV MSGs,
we list the values of all symmetry invariants,
where MSG-realizable in BLUE, and only SSG-realizable in RED.
The BLACK sets of invariants cannot be realized by either MSGs or SSGs.
We find additional 108 classes of new quasiparticles with type III magnetic point group symmetries only realizable in the electronic bands of SSGs.
If the minimal dimension of irReps of the same class is FOUR or higher,
we provide the degree of degeneracy, the lowest-order dispersion, and the direction of nodal lines. An example is a four-fold degenerate 7-nodal-line-nexus fermion protected by
T_{d}\times Z_{2}^{IT}
(No.57 type III magnetic point group in Supplementary Table
S27
), only realizable in SSGs, with three nodal lines along
k_{x},k_{y},k_{z}
directions and four along
k_{x}+k_{y}+k_{z}
,
k_{x}+k_{y}-k_{z}
,
k_{x}-k_{y}+k_{z}
, and
k_{x}-k_{y}-k_{z}
directions.

S8.2
Coplanar and collinear SSGs with nontrivial spin only groups
S_{0}

The spin only group
S_{0}
may have two consequences:

(I)
S_{0}
may give rise to extra nontrivial symmetry invariants;

(II)
S_{0}
may change (enlarge) the dimensions of some projective irReps, even if no nontrivial values of invariants are introduced by
S_{0}
.

More symmetry invariants.
Now we address the issue about the symmetry invariants for little co-groups with nontrivial spin only group
S_{0}
.
The little co-group
G_{\mathbf{K}}
at momentum
\bf K
has the following structure,

\displaystyle G_{\mathbf{K}}/S_{0\mathbf{K}}\cong M^{\prime}_{\mathbf{K}}

(S37)

where
S_{0\mathbf{K}}
is the spin-only group at
\mathbf{K}
(which is not necesarily the same with
S_{0}
since anti-unitary elements in
S_{0}
reverse the lattice momentum) and
M^{\prime}_{\mathbf{K}}
stands for the group formed by pure lattice operations or ‘spin associated’ lattice operations. In most cases, the little co-group
G_{\mathbf{K}}
is a direct product group with

G_{\bf K}=S_{0\bf K}\times M^{\prime}_{\bf K}
.
In other cases,
G_{\mathbf{K}}
is a semidirect product group
G_{\bf K}=S_{0\bf K}\rtimes M^{\prime}_{\bf K}
.

For unitary product groups of the form
G_{1}\times G_{2}
, we have the Kunneth formula (see SRef.
Hung and Wen 2013
)

\mathcal{H}^{d}(G_{1}\times G_{2},\mathrm{U}(1))=\bigoplus\limits_{p=0}^{d}\mathcal{H}^{d-p}[G_{1},\mathcal{H}^{p}(G_{2},\mathrm{U}(1))]
,
where
\mathcal{H}^{d}(G,\mathcal{A})
is
d
th group cohomology with
\mathcal{A}
-coefficient
(usually the Abelian group
\mathcal{A}
is a subgroup of
\mathrm{U}(1)
).
Similar formula exists for semidirect product groups
G_{1}\rtimes G_{2}
. For
d=2
, one has

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{2}(G_{1}\times G_{2},\mathrm{U}(1))=\mathcal{H}^{2}(G_{1},\mathrm{U}(1))\oplus\mathcal{H}^{2}(G_{2},\mathrm{U}(1))\oplus\mathcal{H}^{1}(G_{1},\mathcal{H}^{1}(G_{2},\mathrm{U}(1))).

(S38)

With slight modifications, this formular can be applied to anti-unitary groups, for instance,

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{2}(G\times Z_{2}^{T},\mathrm{U}(1))=\mathcal{H}^{2}(Z_{2}^{T},\mathrm{U}(1))\oplus\mathcal{H}^{1}[\mathcal{H}^{2}(G,\mathrm{U}(1)),Z_{2}]\oplus\mathcal{H}^{1}(G,Z_{2}).

(S39)

Generally, for the group
G_{\bf K}=S_{0\bf K}\times M^{\prime}_{\bf K}
or
G_{\bf K}=S_{0\bf K}\rtimes M^{\prime}_{\bf K}
,
with
G_{\bf K}
either unitary or anti-unitary, the symmetry invariants contain three parts:

(1) invariants of
M^{\prime}_{\mathbf{K}}
from
\mathcal{H}^{2}(M^{\prime}_{\mathbf{K}},\mathrm{U}(1))
,

(2) invariants of
S_{0\mathbf{K}}
from
\mathcal{H}^{2}(S_{0\mathbf{K}},\mathrm{U}(1))
,

(3) mutual invariants involving both
M^{\prime}_{\mathbf{K}}
and
S_{0\mathbf{K}}
.

In the following we discuss possible new invariants related to
S_{0}
, and will discuss the collinear and coplanar SSGs separately.

(I), Collinear SSG
\mathcal{G}
with
S_{0}=\mathrm{SO}(2)\rtimes Z_{2}^{C_{2x}T}

(a)

(T||T|\boldsymbol{\tau})\in\mathcal{G}
, with the fractional translation
\boldsymbol{\tau}\neq\mathbf{0}
.

Noticing that
(T||T|\boldsymbol{\tau})
acts on both lattice momentum and spin, and that

(T||T|\boldsymbol{\tau})\cdot(C_{2x}T||T|\mathbf{0})=(C_{2x}||E|\boldsymbol{\tau})
, we can choose the spin-only group at
\bf K
as the unitary group

\displaystyle S_{0\bf K}=\mathrm{SO}(2)\rtimes\mathscr{C}_{2x}.

(S40)

Then we have

\mathcal{H}^{2}(S_{0\bf K},\mathrm{U}(1))=\mathbb{Z}_{2},

the corresponding invariant is

\displaystyle\eta_{x,z}=\eta^{S}_{x,z}=(\hat{C}_{2x}||E)(\hat{C}_{2z}||E)(\hat{C}_{2x}||E)^{-1}(\hat{C}_{2z}||E)^{-1}=(-1)^{2s},

(S41)

where
(C_{2z}||E)\in S_{0\bf K}
,
the hatted quantity are the representation of operators acting on single-particle Hilbert space,

s
is spin quantum number.
For spin-1/2, one has
\eta^{S}_{x,z}=-1
, indicating the 2-fold degeneracy in the whole BZ in the spin sector. This case belongs to the conventional antiferromagnetism(AFM).

For simplicity, we denote
M^{\prime}_{\bf K}
as the group formed by pure lattice operations (except for anti-unitary elements which act on spin in the same way as
T
), then
G_{\bf K}=S_{0\bf K}\times M^{\prime}_{\bf K}
. Since
T
acts on both lattice and spin degrees of freedom, there may exist some nontrivial mutual invariants
contributed from
S_{0\bf K}
and
M^{\prime}_{\bf K}
. For instance, for

(C_{2m}||E)\in S_{0\bf K}
with
m=x,y,z
, an SSG operation
(C_{2m}T||T|\boldsymbol{\tau})
,
the mutual invariant

\displaystyle\eta_{(C_{2m}T||T)}=(\hat{C}_{2m}\hat{T}||\hat{T})^{2}=\eta_{(C_{2m}T||T)}^{S}\eta_{(C_{2m}T||T)}^{L}=\eta^{S}_{C_{2m}T}\eta_{T}^{L}=\eta_{T}^{L}

(S42)

may take nontrivial value, here we have used the result

\eta^{S}_{C_{2m}T}=(\hat{C}_{2m}\hat{T})^{2}=1

for both integer and half-odd-integer spin,
and
\eta^{L}_{T}=\exp(-i\mathbf{K}_{T}\cdot\boldsymbol{\tau})
from
Eq.(
9
) of the main text.
Hence all nontrivial mutual invariants are originating from the lattice invariant

\eta_{T}^{L}
.
Notice that the invariant

\eta_{(C_{2m}T||T)}=\eta_{T}^{L}

is not the same as the invariant
\eta_{(T||T)}

since

\eta_{(T||T)}=(\hat{T}||\hat{T})^{2}=\eta_{T}^{S}\eta_{T}^{L}=(\hat{T})^{2}\eta_{T}^{L}=(-1)^{2s}\eta_{T}^{L}
.

(b)

(T||IT|\boldsymbol{\tau})\in\mathcal{G}
with
|\boldsymbol{\tau}|\geq 0
.

In the operation
(T||IT|\boldsymbol{\tau})
, spacial inversion

I
commutes with all the other spatial operations and it acts trivially on the spin sector, the discussion in case (a) can be generalized to the case
(T||IT|\boldsymbol{\tau})\in\mathcal{G}
. Since

(T||IT|\boldsymbol{\tau})\cdot(C_{2x}T||T|\mathbf{0})=(C_{2x}||I|\boldsymbol{\tau})
, we just define the ‘spin-only group’ at
\bf K
as

\displaystyle S_{0\bf K}=\mathrm{SO}(2)\rtimes\mathscr{C}^{\prime}_{2x}

(S43)

with
\mathscr{C}^{\prime}_{2x}=\{E,(C_{2x}||I)\}
. Then we still have

\mathcal{H}^{2}(S_{0\bf K},\mathrm{U}(1))=\mathbb{Z}_{2},

with the invariant

\displaystyle\eta_{x,z}=(\hat{C}_{2x}||\hat{I})(\hat{C}_{2z}||E)(\hat{C}_{2x}||\hat{I})^{-1}(\hat{C}_{2z}||E)^{-1}=(-1)^{2s}.

(S44)

For spin-1/2,
\eta_{x,z}=-1
, one also has 2-fold degeneracy in the whole BZ in the spin sector, which also
belongs to the conventional AFM.
Notice that the spin degeneracy is also characterized by

\eta_{IT}=(\hat{T}||\hat{I}\hat{T})^{2}=\eta_{T}^{S}\eta_{IT}^{L}=\eta_{T}^{S}=\hat{T}^{2}=(-1)^{2s}=\eta_{x,z}=-1

since
\eta^{L}_{IT}=\exp(-i\mathbf{K}_{IT}\cdot\boldsymbol{\tau})=1
from
Eq.(
9
) of the main text.

We also denote
M^{\prime}_{\bf K}
as the group formed by pure lattice operations (except for anti-unitary elements which act on spin in the same way as
T
), then we still have the structure
G_{\bf K}=S_{0\bf K}\times M^{\prime}_{\bf K}
. There are no nontrivial mutual invariants in this case.

(c)

(T||T|\boldsymbol{\tau})\notin\mathcal{G}
and
(T||IT|\boldsymbol{\tau})\notin\mathcal{G}
.

If
\bf K
is a time-reversal-invariant momentum(TRIM), then we choose

\displaystyle S_{0\bf K}=\mathrm{SO}(2)\rtimes Z_{2}^{C_{2x}T}=S_{0}.

(S45)

In this case we have
\mathcal{H}^{2}(S_{0\bf K},\mathrm{U}(1))=\mathbb{Z}_{2}

which is generated by the symmetry invariant

\displaystyle\eta_{(C_{2x}T||T)}=(\hat{C}_{2x}\hat{T}||\hat{T})^{2}=\eta_{C_{2x}T}^{S}\eta_{T}^{L}=\eta_{C_{2x}T}^{S}=1,

(S46)

which always takes trivial values for both integer and half-odd-integer spins.
Here,
\eta_{T}^{L}=1
from Eq.(
9
) of the main text because the fractional translation of
T
is
\mathbf{0}
.

If
\bf K
is not a TRIM, we choose

\displaystyle S_{0\bf K}=\mathrm{SO}(2).

(S47)

In this case we have
\mathcal{H}^{2}(S_{0\bf K},\mathrm{U}(1))=\mathbb{Z}_{1}
, so there is no invariants for
S_{0\bf K}
alone, indicating that the Rep in the spin sector
belongs to the trivial class and generally no degeneracy is protected.

Hence, spin splitting exists for general points in the BZ except for some special points (see below).
Especially, if
\tilde{g}=(C_{2x}||g|\boldsymbol{\tau}_{g})\in\mathcal{G}
where
g\neq I
is unitary, then the magnetic conuration is antiferromagnetic and corresponds to the
altermagnetism
(ALM) in literature.
Otherwise, if such
\tilde{g}
does not exist, then the magnetic conuration is either ferromagnetic or ferrimagnetic.

For momentum
\bf K
no matter TRIM or not,
we denote
M^{\prime}_{\bf K}
as the group formed by ‘spin associated’ lattice operations. In the following we discuss two different situations of
M^{\prime}_{\bf K}
.

(1)
M^{\prime}_{\bf K}
does not contain
C_{2x}
spin operation. In this case, we still have
G_{\bf K}=S_{0\bf K}\times M^{\prime}_{\bf K}
. There are no nontrivial mutual invariants.

(2)
M^{\prime}_{\bf K}
contains
C_{2x}
spin operation. In this case
G_{\bf K}=S_{0\bf K}\rtimes M^{\prime}_{\bf K}
. If there exists an element
g^{\prime}=(C_{2x}||g)\in M^{\prime}_{\bf K}
with
g
unitary (
\bf K
is invariant under the action of
g
),
then for
(C_{2z}||E)\in S_{0\bf K}
there exists nontrivial mutual invariant

\displaystyle\eta_{(C_{2x}||g),(C_{2z}||E)}=(\hat{C}_{2x}||\hat{g})(\hat{C}_{2z}||E)(\hat{C}_{2x}||\hat{g}|)^{-1}(\hat{C}_{2z}||E)^{-1}=(-1)^{2s}.

(S48)

This nontrivial invariant protects the spin degeneracy at
\bf K
for
s=1/2
quasiparticles.
Otherwise, there are not nontrivial mutual invariants.

(II), Coplanar SSG
\mathcal{G}
with
S_{0}=Z_{2}^{C_{2z}T}

In this case
M^{\prime}_{\bf K}
denotes the group formed by ‘spin associated’ lattice operations.

(a)

(T||T|\boldsymbol{\tau})\in\mathcal{G}
with the fractional translation
\boldsymbol{\tau}\neq\mathbf{0}
.

In this case, from
(T||T|\boldsymbol{\tau})\cdot(C_{2z}T||T|\mathbf{0})=(C_{2z}||E|\boldsymbol{\tau})
we can define the spin-only group at
\bf K
as a unitary group

\displaystyle S_{0\bf K}=\mathscr{C}_{2z}=\{E,(C_{2z}||E)\}.

(S49)

Since
\mathcal{H}^{2}(S_{0\bf K},\mathrm{U}(1))=\mathbb{Z}_{1}
, there are no symmetry invariant for
S_{0\bf K}
only.

If the SSG contains other spin operations such as
C_{2x}
or
C_{2y}
(namely if there exist some lattice operation
g
such that
(C_{2x}||g|)\in M^{\prime}_{\bf K}
or
(C_{2y}||g)\in M^{\prime}_{\bf K}
), then there will exist nontrivial mutual invariant:

\displaystyle\eta_{(C_{2x}||g),(C_{2z}||E)}=(\hat{C}_{2x}||\hat{g})(\hat{C}_{2z}||E)(\hat{C}_{2x}||\hat{g})^{-1}(\hat{C}_{2z}||E)^{-1}=\eta_{x,z}^{S}=(-1)^{2s},

(S50)

which originates from the spin invariant
\eta^{S}_{x,z}
and takes nontrivial value
(-1)^{2s}=-1
for spin-1/2.

(b)

(T||IT|\boldsymbol{\tau})\in\mathcal{G}
with
|\boldsymbol{\tau}|\geq 0
.

In this case, from
(T||IT|\boldsymbol{\tau})\cdot(C_{2z}T||T|\mathbf{0})=(C_{2z}||I|\boldsymbol{\tau})
we can define the spin-only group at
\bf K
as a unitary group

\displaystyle S_{0\bf K}=\mathscr{C}^{\prime}_{2z}=\{E,(C_{2z}||I)\}.

(S51)

Since
\mathcal{H}^{2}(S_{0\bf K},\mathrm{U}(1))=\mathbb{Z}_{1}
, there are no symmetry invariants for
S_{0\bf K}
only.

If the SSG contains other spin operations such as
C_{2x}
or
C_{2y}
(namely if there exist some lattice operation
g
such that

(C_{2x}||g)\in M^{\prime}_{\bf K}
or
(C_{2y}||g)\in M^{\prime}_{\bf K}
), then there will exist nontrivial mutual invariant

\displaystyle\eta_{(C_{2x}||g),(C_{2z}||I)}=(\hat{C}_{2x}||\hat{g})(\hat{C}_{2z}||\hat{I})(\hat{C}_{2x}||\hat{g})^{-1}(\hat{C}_{2z}||\hat{I})^{-1}=\eta_{x,z}^{S}\eta_{I,g}^{L}=(-1)^{2s}\eta_{I,g}^{L},

(S52)

which may contain contribution from both spin and lattice invariants.
Here
\eta_{I,g}^{L}
can be calculated from Eq.(
8
) of the main text.

(c)

(T||T|\boldsymbol{\tau})\notin\mathcal{G}
and
(T||IT|\boldsymbol{\tau})\notin\mathcal{G}
.

If
\bf K
is not a time-reversal-invariant point, then

\displaystyle S_{0\bf K}=\{E\}

(S53)

is trivial. So the symmetry invariants are completely determined by
M^{\prime}_{\bf K}
.

If
\bf K
is a time-reversal-invariant point, we can choose

\displaystyle S_{0\bf K}=S_{0}=Z_{2}^{C_{2z}T}.

(S54)

In this case
\mathcal{H}^{2}(S_{0\bf K},\mathrm{U}(1))=\mathbb{Z}_{2}
,
but the corresponding symmetry invariant

\displaystyle\eta_{(C_{2z}T||T)}=(\hat{C}_{2z}\hat{T}||\hat{T})^{2}=\eta_{C_{2z}T}^{S}\eta_{T}^{L}=\eta_{C_{2z}T}^{S}=1,

(S55)

which always takes trivial value for both integer and half-odd-integer spins.
Here,
\eta_{T}^{L}=1
from Eq.(
9
) of the main text because the fractional translation of
T
is
\mathbf{0}
.
If there is a pure lattice operation
(E||g)\in M^{\prime}_{\bf K}
with
g^{2}=E
, from
(E||g|\boldsymbol{\tau}_{g})(C_{2z}T||T|\mathbf{0})=(C_{2z}T||gT|\boldsymbol{\tau}_{g})
,
then there exists nontrivial mutual invariant

\displaystyle\eta_{(C_{2z}T||gT)}=(\hat{C}_{2z}\hat{T}||\hat{g}\hat{T})^{2}=\eta_{C_{2z}T}^{S}\eta_{gT}^{L}=\eta_{gT}^{L},

(S56)

which originates from the lattice invariant
\eta_{gT}^{L}=\exp(-i\mathbf{K}_{gT}\cdot\boldsymbol{\tau}_{g})
from
Eq.(
9
) of the main text.

(III), Non-magnetic SSG
\mathcal{G}
with no SOC and with
S_{0}=\mathrm{SO}(3)

In this case, the spin sector and the lattice (orbital) sector are completely separated. The spin sector has
(2s+1)
-fold degeneracy, and all the other symmetry invariants of unitary symmetry elements are contributed from the non-symmorphic lattice operations. The spin
\mathrm{SO}(3)
group (for
s=1/2
it is actually a
\mathrm{SU}(2)
group) may indeed have nontrivial physical consequence. An example is given in the main text when comparing the 12-fold fermions with or without
\mathrm{SU}(2)
spin symmetry.

In summary, introducing of
S_{0}
can give rise to new symmetry invariants with nontrivial values: (I) in collinear SSGs for conventional antiferromagnets, invariant from
S_{0\bf K}
alone can take nontrivial value, giving rise to spin degeneracy in the whole Brillouin zone; (II) generally there may exist mutual invariants from
S_{0\bf K}
and
M^{\prime}_{\bf K}
taking nontrivial values.

Higher dimensional irReps.
Since
S_{0}
enlarges the group, the dimension of irReps may be enlarged even if
the symmetry invariants remain unchanged. For instance, the three SSGs

229.2.1.9
(non-coplanar),
229.2.1.6.P
(coplanar) and
229.2.1.3.L
(collinear)
differ by their spin-only groups, namely
{S_{0}=\{E\}}
,
{S_{0}^{\prime}=Z_{2}^{C_{2z}T}=\{E,{C_{2z}T}\}}
and
{S_{0}^{\prime\prime}=\mathrm{SO}(2)\rtimes Z_{2}^{C_{2x}T}}
respectively.
At the
R=(\pi,\pi,\pi)
point, the little co-groups of the three SSGs are
G_{\mathbf{K}}={O_{h}\times Z_{2}^{T}},\ {O_{h}\times Z_{2}^{T}\times S_{0}^{\prime}}
and
{O_{h}\times Z_{2}^{T}\times S_{0}^{\prime\prime}}
respectively. As far as the common subgroup
O_{h}\times Z_{2}^{T}
is concerned, the five symmetry invariants
(\eta_{C_{2x},C_{2y}},\eta_{T},\eta_{IT},\eta_{TC_{2a}},\eta_{I,C_{2a}})
take the same values
(+1,+1,-1,-1,-1)
in the three SSGs. However, at the
R
point the irReps for
O_{h}\times Z_{2}^{T}
are not the same for the three SSGs: the group 229.2.1.9 has 12-dimensional (12-D) and 4-D irReps, but both of 229.2.1.6.P and 229.2.1.3.L have 12-D, 8-D and 4-D irReps. The three groups all have a 12-D irRep and have the same
k\cdot p
theory around the 12-fold degenerate point (
R
point).

Table S1:
The definitions of symmetry invariants of
G=P\times Z^{T}_{2}
are given in terms of factor system
\omega
,
where
P
is any one of the 32 crystallographic point groups,

I={\rm spacial\ inversion}
,
M={\rm mirror\ reflection\ plane}
,
T={\rm time\ reversal}
,

Z_{2}^{T}=\{E,T\}
.
The invariants are interpreted as the following:
\eta_{T}\equiv\omega_{2}(T,T)
,

\eta_{IT}\equiv\omega_{2}(IT,IT)
,

\eta_{TC_{2}}\equiv\omega_{2}(TC_{2},TC_{2})
,

\eta_{TM}\equiv\omega_{2}(TM,TM)
,

\eta_{I,C_{2z}}\equiv\frac{\omega_{2}(I,C_{2z})}{\omega_{2}(C_{2z},I)}
,

\eta_{I,C_{2x}}\equiv\frac{\omega_{2}(I,C_{2x})}{\omega_{2}(C_{2x},I)}
,

\eta_{I,C_{2y}}\equiv\frac{\omega_{2}(I,C_{2y})}{\omega_{2}(C_{2y},I)}
,

\eta_{I,C_{2a}}\equiv\frac{\omega_{2}(I,C_{2a})}{\omega_{2}(C_{2a},I)}
,

\eta_{I,C^{\prime}_{21}}\equiv\frac{\omega_{2}(I,C^{\prime}_{21})}{\omega_{2}(C^{\prime}_{21},I)}
,

\eta_{I,C^{\prime\prime}_{21}}\equiv\frac{\omega_{2}(I,C^{\prime\prime}_{21})}{\omega_{2}(C^{\prime\prime}_{21},I)}
,

\eta_{I,C^{+}_{4z}}\equiv\frac{\omega_{2}(I,C^{+}_{4z})}{\omega_{2}(C^{+}_{4z},I)}
,

\eta_{I,C_{2}}\equiv\frac{\omega_{2}(I,C_{2})}{\omega_{2}(C_{2},I)}
,

\eta_{C_{2x},C_{2y}}\equiv\frac{\omega_{2}(C_{2x},C_{2y})}{\omega_{2}(C_{2y},C_{2x})}
,

\eta_{M_{x},M_{y}}\equiv\frac{\omega_{2}(M_{x},M_{y})}{\omega_{2}(M_{y},M_{x})}
,

\eta_{C_{2},C^{\prime}_{21}}\equiv\frac{\omega_{2}(C_{2},C^{\prime}_{21})}{\omega_{2}(C^{\prime}_{21},C_{2})}
,

\eta_{C_{2},M_{d1}}\equiv\frac{\omega_{2}(C_{2},M_{d1})}{\omega_{2}(M_{d1},C_{2})}
,

\eta_{M_{h},C^{\prime}_{21}}\equiv\frac{\omega_{2}(M_{h},C^{\prime}_{21})}{\omega_{2}(C^{\prime}_{21},M_{h})}
,

\eta_{M_{h},M_{d1}}\equiv\frac{\omega_{2}(M_{h},M_{d1})}{\omega_{2}(M_{d1},M_{h})}
,

\eta_{C^{\prime}_{21},C^{\prime\prime}_{21}}\equiv\frac{\omega_{2}(C^{\prime}_{21},C^{\prime\prime}_{21})}{\omega_{2}(C^{\prime\prime}_{21},C^{\prime}_{21})}
.

Table S2:
Lattice invariants of
G_{\mathbf{K}}\cong M_{\mathbf{K}}=P\times Z_{2}^{T}

with
P=C_{3h,4h,6h},D_{3,4,6,3d,3h,4h,6h},O,O_{h}
. Here
G_{\mathbf{K}}
and
M_{\mathbf{K}}
stand for the little co-groups of SSG
\mathcal{G}
and type IV MSG
\mathcal{M}
respectively, with
\mathcal{M}
the lattice part of
\mathcal{G}
. It follows that
\mathcal{G}\cong\mathcal{M}
and
M_{\mathbf{K}}\cong{G}_{\mathbf{K}}
.
The lattice invariants
\vec{\eta}^{L}
of
G_{\mathbf{K}}
are same as those of
M_{\mathbf{K}}
, which can be computed as
\eta^{L}_{p_{1},p_{2}}=e^{-i(\mathbf{K}_{p_{1}}\cdot\mathbf{t}_{p_{2}}-\mathbf{K}_{p_{2}}\cdot\mathbf{t}_{p_{1}})}
if
p_{1},p_{2}
are unitary with
p_{1}p_{2}=p_{2}p_{1}
and
\eta^{L}_{p}=e^{-i\mathbf{K}_{p}\cdot\mathbf{t}_{p}}
if
p
is anti-unitary with
p^{2}=E
,
and
\omega_{2}(g_{1},g_{2})\equiv e^{-i\mathbf{K}_{g_{1}}\cdot\mathbf{t}_{g_{2}}}(g_{1},g_{2}\in M_{\mathbf{K}})

is the factor system of projective Rep of
M_{\mathbf{K}}
at high-symmetry point in BZ
Yang et al. 2021a
.

Table S5:
For (
G_{\mathbf{K}}\cong M_{\mathbf{K}}=D_{4h}\times{Z}_{2}^{T},L_{0\mathbf{K}},M_{\mathbf{K}}/L_{0\mathbf{K}}
),
we list generators of spin point group
(\varphi_{g}||g_{1},\ldots,g_{n})
,
(g_{1},\ldots,g_{n})
is a coset of
L_{0\mathbf{K}}
,

g\in(g_{1},\ldots,g_{n}),\varphi_{g}\in M_{\mathbf{K}}/L_{0\mathbf{K}}
,

n
is order of
L_{0\mathbf{K}}
, and
spin invariants
\vec{\eta}^{S}
of
G_{\mathbf{K}}
is

\eta^{S}_{p_{1},p_{2}}{\cdot 1_{2\times 2}}=d({\varphi_{p_{1}}})d({\varphi_{p_{2}}})d^{-1}({\varphi_{p_{1}}})d^{-1}({\varphi_{p_{2}}})

for unitary elements
p_{1},p_{2}
with
p_{1}p_{2}=p_{2}p_{1}
and

\eta^{S}_{p}{\cdot 1_{2\times 2}}=[d(\varphi_{p}){K}]^{2}

for anti-unitary element
p
with
p^{2}=E
,
where irRep of
g\in G_{\mathbf{K}}
is given by

d(\varphi_{g})K^{\zeta_{g}}=u\Big(s(T^{\zeta_{g}}\varphi_{g})\Big)(i\sigma_{y}K)^{\zeta_{g}}
,

s(T^{\zeta_{g}}\varphi_{g})
is vector Rep of
T^{\zeta_{g}}\varphi_{g}\in\mathrm{SO(3)}
,

u\Big(s(T^{\zeta_{g}}\varphi_{g})\Big)
is
\mathrm{SU(2)}
Rep of
T^{\zeta_{g}}\varphi_{g}
,

\zeta_{g}=0(1)
for unitary (anti-unitary)
g
.
The
\vec{\eta}^{S}
leading to invariants only realized in SSG are labeled by Boolean vectors
colored in
RED
.

p_{1},p_{2}

p^{2}=E
,

g\in G_{\mathbf{K}}

,

s(T^{\zeta_{g}}\varphi_{g})

(g_{1},\ldots,g_{n})

,

L_{0\mathbf{K}}
, and
spin invariants
\vec{\eta}^{S}
of
G_{\mathbf{K}}
is

\eta^{S}_{p_{1},p_{2}}{\cdot 1_{2\times 2}}=d({\varphi_{p_{1}}})d({\varphi_{p_{2}}})d^{-1}({\varphi_{p_{1}}})d^{-1}({\varphi_{p_{2}}})

for unitary elements
p_{1},p_{2}
with
p_{1}p_{2}=p_{2}p_{1}
and

\eta^{S}_{p}{\cdot 1_{2\times 2}}=[d(\varphi_{p}){K}]^{2}

for anti-unitary element
p
with
p^{2}=E
,
where irRep of

,

k

Table S27:
The definitions of symmetry invariants of 58 type III magnetic point groups
are given in terms of factor system
\omega
or irRep
\rho
,
where

I={\rm spacial\ inversion}
,
M={\rm mirror\ reflection\ plane}
,
T={\rm time\ reversal}
,
Z_{2}^{IT}=\{E,IT\}
,
Z_{2}^{T_{c}}=\{E,TC_{2}\}
,
Z_{2}^{T_{m}}=\{E,TM\}
.
The invariants are interpreted as the following:

\eta_{IT}\equiv\omega_{2}(IT,IT)
,

\eta_{TC_{2}}\equiv\omega_{2}(TC_{2},TC_{2})
,

\eta_{TM}\equiv\omega_{2}(TM,TM)
,

\eta_{I,C_{2z}}\equiv\frac{\omega_{2}(I,C_{2z})}{\omega_{2}(C_{2z},I)}
,

\eta_{I,C_{2x}}\equiv\frac{\omega_{2}(I,C_{2x})}{\omega_{2}(C_{2x},I)}
,

\eta_{I,C^{+}_{4z}}\equiv\frac{\omega_{2}(I,C^{+}_{4z})}{\omega_{2}(C^{+}_{4z},I)}
,

\eta_{I,C_{2}}\equiv\frac{\omega_{2}(I,C_{2})}{\omega_{2}(C_{2},I)}
,

\eta_{C_{2x},C_{2y}}\equiv\frac{\omega_{2}(C_{2x},C_{2y})}{\omega_{2}(C_{2y},C_{2x})}
,

\eta_{M_{x},M_{y}}\equiv\frac{\omega_{2}(M_{x},M_{y})}{\omega_{2}(M_{y},M_{x})}
,

\eta_{C_{2},C^{\prime}_{21}}\equiv\frac{\omega_{2}(C_{2},C^{\prime}_{21})}{\omega_{2}(C^{\prime}_{21},C_{2})}
,

\eta_{C_{2},M_{d1}}\equiv\frac{\omega_{2}(C_{2},M_{d1})}{\omega_{2}(M_{d1},C_{2})}
,

\eta_{M_{h},C^{\prime}_{21}}\equiv\frac{\omega_{2}(M_{h},C^{\prime}_{21})}{\omega_{2}(C^{\prime}_{21},M_{h})}
,

\eta_{M_{h},M_{d1}}\equiv\frac{\omega_{2}(M_{h},M_{d1})}{\omega_{2}(M_{d1},M_{h})}
,
\eta_{I,C^{{}^{\prime}}_{21}}\equiv\frac{\omega_{2}(I,C^{{}^{\prime}}_{21})}{\omega_{2}(C^{{}^{\prime}}_{21},I)}
,
\eta_{I,C^{{}^{\prime\prime}}_{21}}\equiv\frac{\omega_{2}(I,C^{{}^{\prime\prime}}_{21})}{\omega_{2}(C^{{}^{\prime\prime}}_{21},I)}
. The Number of
G
is according to Table 7.1 of book
Bradley and Cracknell 2010

For a type III magnetic point group, if not all the sets of symmetry invariants can be realized at high-symmetry points in BZ of MSGs,
we list the values of all symmetry invariants,
where MSG-realizable in
BLUE
, and only SSG-realizable in
RED
. The BLACK sets of invariants cannot be realized in MSG, or even SSG. For only SSG-realizable invariants, dim denotes the lowest dimension of irRep,
disp. are the
k
-terms which can split band degeneracy, n.l. is direction of nodal line in BZ.
